# SSM document-catalog and association-version reads serve synthesized metadata instead of stored state

> _Note: this issue was identified against the code base at commit `76daa3f15e1fe41f6bbe7b45b84d5dc4d4ac931b`. If you are an issue fixer working on this, the code base you are currently working in can have drifted, so you might need to trace the issue back to the current code base._

## Operation
ListDocuments

## Description
3 net-new causes in the ssm crate's read projections for the Amazon-owned document catalog and for State Manager association versions. In each one the emulator answers 200 with a record it synthesizes (one constant creation timestamp, one implicit version, a per-call delta) instead of the per-document / per-version state AWS keeps, so clients that read catalog or version metadata get values that are structurally present but wrong.

Cause 1 of 3: ListDocuments projects Amazon-owned documents with a constant CreatedDate and drops per-document metadata (fixture ssm-catalog-list-documents, step task#1).
AWS: Smithy: ListDocuments output DocumentIdentifiers -> DocumentIdentifierList of DocumentIdentifier{Name, CreatedDate (DateTime), DisplayName, Owner, VersionName, PlatformTypes, DocumentVersion, DocumentType, SchemaVersion, DocumentFormat, TargetType, Tags, Requires, ReviewStatus, Author}. Each Amazon-owned document carries its OWN publication timestamp in CreatedDate, its own DocumentFormat (the AWS-* Automation runbooks published as YAML report 'YAML', the legacy Command documents report 'JSON'), its own SchemaVersion, its own TargetType, its own PlatformTypes (only the platforms that document actually supports), and (recorded behavior only - Categories is not a member of DocumentIdentifier in the on-disk model) a Categories list such as ['InstanceManagement'], ['ConfigurationManagement'] or ['PatchManagement']. Change-template documents additionally carry Author, Description and ReviewStatus.
Emulator: every listed document is projected from one seeded template: CreatedDate is a single constant epoch value shared by the whole catalog (the replay shows the same value for AWS-ASGEnterStandby, AWS-ASGExitStandby, AWS-ApplyChefRecipes, AWS-ApplyDSCMofs, AWS-ApplyPatchBaseline and dozens more, with a second constant used for a few others), Categories is absent where AWS sends a list, DocumentFormat is reported 'JSON' for documents AWS publishes as 'YAML' (AWS-AddOpsItemDedupStringToEventBridgeRule, AWS-ArchiveEBSVolumeSnapshots, AWS-EnableCloudTrail and the whole AWS-Enable*/AWS-Diagnose* family), PlatformTypes carries platforms the document does not support (extra 'Linux'/'MacOS' entries on AWS-ApplyChefRecipes, AWS-ApplyDSCMofs, AWS-ApplyPatchBaseline, AWS-ConfigureWindowsUpdate, AWS-InstallMissingWindowsUpdates, AWS-InstallSpecificWindowsUpdates), SchemaVersion/TargetType/DocumentType diverge on several entries (AWS-ApplyPatchBaseline, AWS-FindWindowsUpdates, AWS-InstallAmazonECSAgent, AWS-PasswordReset, AWS-RunShellScript, AWS-RunPowerShellScript), Author/Description/ReviewStatus are absent on AWS-HelloWorldChangeTemplate, and AWS-ConfigureS3BucketPublicAccessBlock is missing from the catalog entirely.
Why: a client that discovers a runbook by listing and then acts on the listed metadata takes the wrong branch - it fetches a document as JSON that is actually YAML, offers a Windows-only runbook for a Linux target because PlatformTypes claims Linux, filters by Categories and gets an empty result because the member is absent, and cannot order or age documents because every CreatedDate is identical. Open issue 20261119T003828Z-ssm-seeded-built-in-catalogs-are-incomplete-listdocuments.md covers the same catalog but was keyed on the entries that were missing altogether; it carries no replay key for the metadata divergences this step now reports, so this cause is not gated by it.
Emulator fix: the ssm crate's seeded Amazon-owned document catalog (state) must hold each document's own CreatedDate, DocumentFormat, SchemaVersion, DocumentType, TargetType, PlatformTypes, Categories and change-template Author/Description/ReviewStatus, and the ListDocuments response projection must emit them per document rather than filling them from one template.
Traces: ssm-catalog-list-documents#task#1.

Cause 2 of 3: ListDocumentVersions serves a single synthesized version for a multi-version Amazon-owned document (fixture ssm-catalog-list-document-versions, step task#16).
AWS: Smithy: ListDocumentVersions input Name (DocumentARN, required); output DocumentVersions -> DocumentVersionList (@length min 1) of DocumentVersionInfo{Name, DisplayName, DocumentVersion, VersionName, CreatedDate, IsDefaultVersion, DocumentFormat, Status, ReviewStatus}. AWS returns one entry per published version of the named document, newest version first, each with the timestamp at which THAT version was published and IsDefaultVersion true only on the default version. The recording for the Amazon-owned support runbook in this step lists five versions ('5' down to '1'), Status 'Active', DocumentFormat 'JSON', IsDefaultVersion true on the newest.
Emulator: the response carries exactly one DocumentVersionInfo - DocumentVersion '1', IsDefaultVersion true, CreatedDate the same constant epoch placeholder used by the catalog in cause 1 - because the seeded catalog stores no version history for Amazon-owned documents; the four older versions are absent.
Why: a client that pins an automation to a specific document version, or that reads the version history to decide whether to upgrade, sees a document that has only ever had version 1, so a request naming a version AWS publishes is unresolvable and no version-pinning or default-version-drift logic can work. Open issue 20261119T003828Z-ssm-listdocumentversions-reports-wrong-createddate-and-documentformat-for.md is adjacent (it was filed against the wrong CreatedDate/DocumentFormat of a single served version) and does not carry this step's replay key.
Emulator fix: the ssm crate's seeded document catalog (state) must carry a version list per Amazon-owned document, and the ListDocumentVersions handler/projection must emit every stored version with that version's own CreatedDate and IsDefaultVersion rather than fabricating a lone version '1'.
Traces: ssm-catalog-list-document-versions#task#16.

Cause 3 of 3: ListAssociationVersions returns versions oldest-first, each version holding only the members that call changed, and omits CalendarNames (fixture ssm-state-manager-association-workflow, step task#4).
AWS: Smithy: ListAssociationVersions input AssociationId (required); output AssociationVersions -> AssociationVersionList (@length min 1) of AssociationVersionInfo{AssociationId, AssociationVersion, CreatedDate, Name, DocumentVersion, Parameters, Targets, ScheduleExpression, OutputLocation, AssociationName, MaxErrors, MaxConcurrency, ComplianceSeverity, SyncCompliance, ApplyOnlyAtCronInterval, CalendarNames, TargetLocations, ScheduleOffset, Duration, TargetMaps}. Each entry is a complete snapshot of the association's configuration AS OF that version - the member docs read 'the targets specified for the association when the association version was created', 'the cron or rate schedule specified for the association when the association version was created' - so a member that was set in version 1 and not changed by the update still appears in version 2. The recording lists the newest version first (version '2', then version '1') and sends CalendarNames as an empty list on every entry.
Emulator: the list is ordered oldest-first (version '1' at index 0, version '2' at index 1); the version-2 entry carries only the member the update changed (ScheduleExpression) and DROPS Targets, which version 1 recorded and the update did not touch; and CalendarNames is absent from every entry where AWS sends [].
Why: a client that reads element 0 to get the current configuration of an association - the normal way to read 'what is this association doing now' - gets the ORIGINAL version instead of the latest, and a client that diffs or rolls back a version sees Targets as having been cleared by an update that never mentioned them. A client reading CalendarNames gets null instead of an empty list and has to special-case the absent member. Open issue 20261116T061000Z-ssm-ops-center-maintenance-window-and-association-reads.md and 20261117T174748Z-ssm-state-manager-and-patch-baseline-reads-omit-modeled.md are in this area but carry no replay key for this step.
Emulator fix: the ssm crate's association-version state must store each version as the full effective configuration at that version (carrying forward members an update does not change) and the ListAssociationVersions projection must emit the list newest-version-first and include CalendarNames as an empty list when no change calendar gates the association.
Traces: ssm-state-manager-association-workflow#task#4.

## Evidence
Cause 1 - ssm-catalog-list-documents#task#1, ListDocuments, expected 200 / actual 200, cluster key service=ssm|ListDocuments|status=200->200: $.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-ASGEnterStandby"].Categories: missing (expected ['InstanceManagement'], got None); .CreatedDate: value_mismatch (expected the document's own publication timestamp 1518661792.37, got the constant 1451606400); same constant returned for AWS-ASGExitStandby, AWS-ApplyChefRecipes, AWS-ApplyDSCMofs, AWS-ApplyPatchBaseline and dozens of further entries; $.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-AddOpsItemDedupStringToEventBridgeRule"].DocumentFormat: value_mismatch (expected 'YAML', got 'JSON'); $.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-ApplyChefRecipes"].PlatformTypes[2]: extra (expected None, got 'MacOS'); $.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-ApplyDSCMofs"].PlatformTypes[1]/[2]: extra (got 'Linux','MacOS'); $.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-ApplyPatchBaseline"].SchemaVersion and .Categories diverge; $.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-HelloWorldChangeTemplate"].Author/.Description/.ReviewStatus: missing; $.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-ConfigureS3BucketPublicAccessBlock"]: missing (whole entry absent); further Categories/CreatedDate/DocumentFormat/TargetType/SchemaVersion/DocumentType keys across the AWS-Configure*, AWS-Create*, AWS-Delete*, AWS-Diagnose*, AWS-Enable*, AWS-Install*, AWS-Run* families.

Cause 2 - ssm-catalog-list-document-versions#task#16, ListDocumentVersions, expected 200 / actual 200, cluster key service=ssm|ListDocumentVersions|status=200->200|keys=$.DocumentVersions[0..4]: expected five entries for the same Name - {'DocumentVersion': '5', 'IsDefaultVersion': True, 'DocumentFormat': 'JSON', 'Status': 'Active', 'CreatedDate': 1728550923.741}, then '4' (1709719148.098), '3' (1701351959.61), '2' (1537580033.794), '1' (1535478878.887) each with IsDefaultVersion False; got a single entry {'DocumentVersion': '1', 'IsDefaultVersion': True, 'DocumentFormat': 'JSON', 'Status': 'Active', 'CreatedDate': 1451606400} and $.DocumentVersions[1]..[4]: missing (got None).

Cause 3 - ssm-state-manager-association-workflow#task#4, ListAssociationVersions, expected 200 / actual 200, cluster key service=ssm|ListAssociationVersions|status=200->200|keys=$.AssociationVersions[0],$.AssociationVersions[1]: expected [0] = AssociationVersion '2' with ScheduleExpression 'rate(30 minutes)', Targets [{Key 'tag:Environment', Values [...]}], CalendarNames [], ApplyOnlyAtCronInterval False and [1] = AssociationVersion '1' with the same Targets, CalendarNames [] and no ScheduleExpression; got [0] = AssociationVersion '1' (Targets present, CalendarNames absent) and [1] = AssociationVersion '2' (ScheduleExpression present, Targets ABSENT, CalendarNames absent). CreatedDate was not asserted.

## Steps to Reproduce
1. Clone the repository and boot the emulator built from that checkout.
2. Replay the wire fixture corpus (trace set simparity-wire-aws-v1).
3. Cause 1: replay fixture ssm-catalog-list-documents step task#1 - an unfiltered ListDocuments - and compare each DocumentIdentifier's CreatedDate (is it the same constant for every document?), Categories, DocumentFormat, PlatformTypes, SchemaVersion, TargetType, DocumentType and the change-template Author/Description/ReviewStatus against the recording; check that AWS-ConfigureS3BucketPublicAccessBlock is served at all.
4. Cause 2: replay fixture ssm-catalog-list-document-versions step task#16 - ListDocumentVersions for the Amazon-owned support runbook named by the step - and observe one DocumentVersionInfo (version '1', constant CreatedDate) where the recording lists five versions newest-first.
5. Cause 3: replay fixture ssm-state-manager-association-workflow step task#4 - CreateAssociation, then UpdateAssociation changing only the schedule, then ListAssociationVersions for that AssociationId - and observe the list ordered oldest-first, the version-2 entry missing the Targets carried over from version 1, and CalendarNames absent on both entries.
